{"type":"message_start","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Dependency Topological Sort\n\nCreate `src/topo.js` exporting one named ESM function, `order(nodes, edges)`. `nodes` is an array of string ids; `edges` is an array of `[before, after]` pairs meaning `before` must appear before `after` in the result. The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`.\n- `order([\"a\"], [[\"a\",\"a\"]])` throws `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n- With `nodes [\"a\",\"b\",\"c\",\"d\"]` and edges `[[\"d\",\"b\"],[\"c\",\"d\"]]`, the result is `[\"a\",\"c\",\"d\",\"b\"]`.\n\n\nNote: aim for a clean, readable implementation; correctness matters most."}],"timestamp":1794047636885}}

The function returns an array containing every node exactly once, respecting all edge constraints, and never mutates its arguments. Node.js builtins only, no external packages. ESM.\n\nDeterminism: the result is the unique order produced by repeatedly taking, among all nodes whose prerequisites are already emitted and that are not yet emitted, the alphabetically smallest id (plain `<` comparison on the strings). Nodes with no edges participate in this same selection process.\n\nErrors, checked in this order:\n1. Unknown nodes: if any edge endpoint is not found in `nodes` (using exact string equality), throw `new Error(\"unknown node: x\")` where `x` is the first offending endpoint, scanning edges in order and each edge as `[before, after]` (before first, then after). This check runs over all edges before any ordering work and even when the graph is also cyclic. Duplicate entries in `nodes` are not an error; treat the node list as a set. A duplicate edge (the same `[before, after]` pair appearing more than once) imposes the same single constraint as one copy of that edge and is not an error.\n2. Cycles: if the graph has a cycle, throw `new Error(\"cycle detected: a -> b -> a\")`. The message is `\"cycle detected: \"` followed by a cycle path: an actual node id, then `\" -> \"` repeated, ending with that same starting id again, tracing real edges of the graph through distinct nodes (e.g. `b -> c -> b`). Any cycle and any starting node inside it are acceptable — the message must name real ids joined by `\" -> \"`, start and end with the same id, and every step must follow an edge. A self-edge such as `[\"a\", \"a\"]` is a cycle; its message is exactly `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n\nExamples:\n- `order([\"b\",\"a\",\"c\"], [[\"a\",\"b\"]])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`: \"a\" precedes \"b\", and among the free nodes \"a\" and \"c\" the alphabetically smallest is emitted first, then \"b\" and \"c\" compete.\n- `order([\"c\",\"b\",\"a\"], [])` returns `[\"a\",\"b\",\"c\"]`.\n- `order([\"a\"], [[\"a\",\"a\"]])` throws `cycle detected: a -> a`.\n- With `nodes [\"a\",\"b\",\"c\",\"d\"]` and edges `[[\"d\",\"b\"],[\"c\",\"d\"]]`, the result is `[\"a\",\"c\",\"d\",\"b\"]`.\n\n\nNote: aim for a clean, readable implementation; correctness matters most."}],"timestamp":1794047636885}}

Validate: every edge endpoint must be a known node.\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    if (!nodeSet.has(before)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${before}`);\n    if (!nodeSet.has(after)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${after}`);\n  }\n\n  // Build adjacency (before -> afters) and prerequisites count. Validate: every edge endpoint must be a known node.\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    if (!nodeSet.has(before)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${before}`);\n    if (!nodeSet.has(after)) throw new Error(`unknown node: ${after}`);\n  }\n\n  // Build adjacency (before -> afters) and prerequisites count. Duplicate edges\n  // impose the same single constraint, so use a per-node set to dedupe.\n  const successors = new Map();\n  const prerequisites = new Map();\n  for (const node of nodes) {\n    successors.set(node, new Set());\n    prerequisites.set(node, 0);\n  }\n  for (const [before, after] of edges) {\n    const set = successors.get(before);\n    if (!set.has(after)) {\n      set.add(after);\n      prerequisites.set(after, prerequisites.get(after) + 1);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // 2. Detect cycles: DFS over real edges through distinct nodes.\n  const state = new Map(nodes.map((n) => [n, 0])); // 0 = unvisited, 1 = visiting, 2 = done\n  const pathStack = [];\n\n  function detectCycle(start) {\n    pathStack.push(start);\n    state.set(start, 1);\n\n    for (const next of successors.get(start)) {\n      const st = state.get(next);\n      if (st === 0) {\n        if (detectCycle(next)) return true;\n      } else if (st === 1) {\n        // Found a cycle: trim the stack to start at `next`.\n        const idx = pathStack.indexOf(next);\n        const cycle = pathStack.slice(idx).concat(next);\n        throw new Error(`cycle detected: ${cycle.join(\" -> \")}`);\n      }\n    }\n\n    state.set(start, 2);\n    pathStack.pop();\n    return false;\n  }\n\n  for (const node of nodes) {\n    if (state.get(node) === 0) {\n      if (detectCycle(node)) return; // unreachable (throw happens above)\n    }\n  }\n\n  // 3. Kahn's algorithm: repeatedly pick the smallest free node.\n  const available = [];\n  for (const node of nodes) {\n    if (prerequisites.get(node) === 0) available.push(node);\n  }\n\n  const result = [];\n  const remaining = new Map(prerequisites);\n\n  while (available.length > 0) {\n    // Grab the alphabetically smallest available node.\n    let minIdx = 0;\n    for (let i = 1; i < available.length; i++) {\n      if (available[i] < available[minIdx]) minIdx = i;\n    }\n    const current = available.splice(minIdx, 1)[0];\n    result.push(current);\n\n    for (const next of successors.get(current)) {\n      const count = remaining.get(next) - 1;\n      remaining.set(next, count);\n      if (count === 0) available.push(next);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // We validated no cycles, so result covers every node.\n  return result;\n}"}]},"isError":false}
